Example: Implementing the Electrothermal Resistor Model

       This section presents the equations for the electrothermal resistor model.

       Note:
           This model is also available as a built-in model in Sentaurus Device (see
           Electrothermal Resistor (Ter) Model on page 158).

Model Equations

An electrothermal resistance has three contacts (two electrodes and one thermode):

                                                                     t1

                           u1  i                  R      u2

The electrical behavior of the resistance is described by Ohm's law:

           u1 - u2 = R  i                                                             (48)

The resistance of the device, however, depends on the thermode temperature:

           R = r  1 +   t1 - tref +   t1 - tref2                                      (49)

The device also produces Joule heat, which is dissipated through the thermode:

           P = u1 - u2  i                                                             (50)

The device can be described by the following vector of unknowns:

                     u1                                                               (51)

           zt = u2
                      t1

                      i

Compact Models User Guide                                                             203

W-2024.09
Chapter 4: Compact Model Interface in Sentaurus Device
Example: Implementing the Electrothermal Resistor Model

The DC right-hand side is given by:

           fRt zt =         i                                                        (52)
                            -i
                     -u1 - u2  i
                     u1 - u2 - R  i

The first two entries of fR correspond to the current flowing into the device through the
electrodes u1 and u2. The third entry of fR represents the heat flow into the device through
the thermode t1.

Equation 48 and Equation 50 have no derivatives with regard to time. Therefore, the
transient right-hand side is zero. The Jacobian of fR is given by:

                            00       0  1
                            00
                            -i i     0  -1

           d fRt zt = Jf =           0 -u1 - u2                                      (53)
           dz        R

                            1 -1 - dR  i -R
                                     dt1

where dR = r   + 2    t1 - tref .
        dt1

tres.ccf                                // input voltage 1
                                        // input voltage 2
 DEVICE tres
       ELECTRODES                       // thermode for Joule heat
            u1
            u2                          // current through resistor
       THERMODES
            t1                          // resistance
       INTERNALS                        // linear temperature coefficient
            i                           // quadratic temperature coefficient
       PARAMETERS                       // reference temperature
            double r = 1
            double alpha = 0
            double beta = 0
            double tref = 300.15

 END DEVICE

 PSET tres_pset
       DEVICE tres

 END PSET

{ Instance* inst = (Instance*) instance->instance_desc;

#define u1 (variables [0])
#define u2 (variables [1])
#define t1 (variables [2])
#define i (variables [3])

extern "C" void
cmi_instance_get_rhs (CCMBaseInstance*const instance,

                                        const int dc, const double time,
                                        const double*const variables,
                                        double*const rhs)
{ Instance* inst = (Instance*) instance->instance_desc;
     if (dc) {
          double dT = t1 - inst->par.tref;
          double R = inst->par.r *
                           (1.0 + (inst->par.alpha + inst->par.beta * dT) * dT);
          rhs [0] = i;
          rhs [1] = -i;
          rhs [2] = -(u1 - u2) * i;
     rhs [3] = u1 - u2 - R * i;
     } else {
          rhs [0] = 0.0;
          rhs [1] = 0.0;
          rhs [2] = 0.0;
     rhs [3] = 0.0;
     }
}

extern "C" void
cmi_instance_get_jacobian (CCMBaseInstance*const instance,

                                                 const int dc, const double time,
                                                 const double*const variables,
                                                 double*const*const jacobian)
{ Instance* inst = (Instance*) instance->instance_desc;
     if (dc) {
          double dT = t1 - inst->par.tref;
          double R = inst->par.r *
                           (1.0 + (inst->par.alpha + inst->par.beta * dT) * dT);
          double dRdt1 = inst->par.r *
                           (inst->par.alpha + 2.0 * inst->par.beta * dT);
          jacobian[0][3] = 1.0;
          jacobian[1][3] = -1.0;
          jacobian[2][0] = -i;
          jacobian[2][1] = i;
          jacobian[2][3] = u2 - u1;
          jacobian[3][0] = 1.0;
          jacobian[3][1] = -1.0;
          jacobian[3][2] = -i*dRdt1;
          jacobian[3][3] = -R;
     }

Compact Models User Guide                                                          208

W-2024.09
Chapter 4: Compact Model Interface in Sentaurus Device
CMI Models With Frequency-Domain Assembly

}

extern "C" int             (CCMBaseInstance*const instance,
cmi_instance_is_physical    const double time,
                            const double*const variables)
{ return 1;
}

{ delete (Instance*) instance->instance_desc;

CMI Models With Frequency-Domain Assembly

       This section describes the provided CMI models suitable for the HB-MDFT mode.

       Notation: In general, time-domain (TD) quantities are denoted by lowercase letters, while
       the corresponding frequency-domain (FD) quantity is denoted by uppercase letters. See
       Frequency-Domain Model Equations on page 173 for the Fourier representation.

       Throughout this section, the following notation is used:

       · ui is the TD voltage at electrode ni.
       · uij:=ui - uj are the voltage differences.
       · ik is the current from electrode nk into the device.

Admittance sd_hb_pGC

The device sd_hb_pGC describes the behavior of a (real-valued) conductance G in parallel
with a (real-valued) capacitance C.

Device name:               sd_hb_pGC

Device parameter set name: sd_hb_pGC_pset

Electrodes:                Two electrodes n1 and n2
Internal variables:        None

Compact Models User Guide                                    209

Chapter 4: Compact Model Interface in Sentaurus Device

                                              0.

                                                                   (54)
        i1t - Gu12t + Cu12 = 0

DC Equation:

           i1 - Gu12 = 0                                           (55)

HB Equation:

For each frequency f  0, you have:

           I1f - GU12f + i2f C U12f = 0                            (56)

Impedance sd_hb_sRL

The device sd_hb_sRL describes the behavior of a (real-valued) resistance R in series with
a (real-valued) inductance L.

Device name:                       sd_hb_sRL

Device parameter set name: sd_hb_sRL_pset

Electrodes:                        Two electrodes n1 and n2
Internal variables:                Current i1 from n1 into device

                                              0.

Transient Equation:

                                                                         (57)
        R i1t + L i1t - u12t = 0

DC Equation:

           R i1 - u12 = 0                                                (58)

HB Equation:

For each frequency f  0 , you have:

           R I1f + i2f L I1f - U12f = 0                                  (59)

Harmonic Voltage Source sd_hb_vsource

The device sd_hb_vsource represents a harmonic voltage source. It can operate in
different modes selected by the dcmode parameter:

· If dcmode=0, then the DC and HB analyses are consistent and the device behaves as a
    constant voltage source in transient analysis.

· If dcmode=1, then the DC behavior is given by the transient description at time t = 0.

Device name:                  sd_hb_vsource

Device parameter set name: sd_hb_vsource_pset

Electrodes:                   Two electrodes n1 and n2
Internal variables:           Current i1 from n1 into device

Table 62 Device parameters

Name       Description            Symbol  Domain        Unit    Default
dcmode     Mode of DC             -       {0,1}         1       0
dc         DC voltage             V0      (-, )         V       0.
freq       Frequency of tone      f1      [0, )         Hz      0.
mag        Magnitude of tone      M1      [0, )         V       0.
phase      Phase of tone          1deg    (-, )         degree  0.

Transient Equations:

                                    u12t - V0 = 0  if dcmode = 0                     (60)
        u12t - V0 + M1 cos 1t + 1 = 0              if dcmode = 1                     (61)

                            u12 - V0 = 0  if dcmode = 0
        u12 - V0 + M1 cos 1 = 0           if dcmode = 1

           U12f - V0 = 0   for f = 0
           U12f - V1 = 0   for f = f1

                 U12f = 0         else

Harmonic Current Source sd_hb_isource

The device sd_hb_isource represents a harmonic current source.

It is derived in complete analogy to the harmonic voltage source sd_hb_vsource (see
Harmonic Voltage Source sd_hb_vsource on page 211).

Multitone Voltage Source sd_hb_vsource2

The device sd_hb_vsource2 represents a multitone voltage source with two tones. It is an
extension of the harmonic voltage source sd_hb_vsource (see Harmonic Voltage Source
sd_hb_vsource on page 211) to two-tone HB analysis by allowing the specification of two
independent tones of oscillation.

Device name:                            sd_hb_vsource2

Device parameter set name: sd_hb_vsource2_pset

Electrodes:                             Two electrodes n1 and n2
Internal variables:                     Current i1 from n1 into device

Syntax of Compact Circuit (.ccf) Files

Syntax of Compact Circuit (.ccf) Files

       The grammar of a compact circuit file can be defined in Backus-Naur form (BNF). The
       symbol  denotes empty production. Terminal symbols are in Courier font. Identifiers are
       sequences of letters and digits. The first character must be a letter. An underscore (_) is also
       considered to be a letter.

CCMStrFile                 

                           | Descriptors

Descriptors                Device

                           | PSet

                           | Instance

                           | Device Descriptors

                           | PSet Descriptors

                           | Instance Descriptors

Device                     DeviceHeader DeviceElectrodes DeviceThermodes
                           DeviceInternals DeviceStates DeviceParams DeviceFooter

DeviceHeader               DEVICE DeviceName

DeviceName                 Identifier

DeviceElectrodes           

                           | ELECTRODES

                           | ELECTRODES DeviceElectrodeList

DeviceElectrodeList        DeviceElectrode

                           | DeviceElectrode DeviceElectrodeList

DeviceElectrode            Identifier

DeviceThermodes            

                           | THERMODES

                           | THERMODES DeviceThermodeList

Compact Models User Guide                                                          216

                             Identifier
DeviceInternalList           
DeviceInternal             | INTERNALS
DeviceStates               | INTERNALS DeviceInternalList
                             DeviceInternal
DeviceStateList            | DeviceInternal DeviceInternalList
DeviceState                  Identifier
StateName                    
                           | STATES
                           | STATES DeviceStateList
                             DeviceState
                           | DeviceState DeviceStateList
                             SimpleType StateName
                           | SimpleType StateName [ Dimension ]
                             Identifier

DeviceParams

DeviceParam                | PARAMETERS DeviceParamList

                             DeviceParam
                           | DeviceParam DeviceParamList

                             SimpleType ParamName

Syntax of Compact Circuit (.ccf) Files

ParamName                    Identifier
ParamValue                   VectorValue
VectorValue                | ScalarValue
ScalarList                   []
ScalarValue                | [ ScalarList ]
                             ScalarValue
                           | ScalarValue ScalarList
                             Character
                           | Integer
                           | Real
                           | String

       In ScalarValue, Table 66 lists the values that are recognized.
Table 66 Scalar values

Type         Value                                                        Example

Character A character enclosed in single quotation marks                  'c'

Integer An optionally signed sequence of digits                           1, +2, -33

Real         An integer, a fixed point value, or a floating-point value as in C++ 1, -2.0, 3.4e-5

String       A string consisting of a sequence of characters enclosed in  "hello world"
             double quotation marks

Real values can also consist of fixed point numbers with a scaling factor as listed in
Table 67.

Table 67 Scaling factors

Scaling factor  Value      Example              Scaling factor  Value  Example
t (tera)        1012       1.2t = 1.2 × 1012    u (micro)       10-6   7u = 7 × 10-6
g (giga)        109        -2.3g = -2.3 × 109   n (nano)        10-9   8n = 8 × 10-9
meg (mega)      106        4meg = 4 × 106       p (pico)        10-12  5.6p = 5.6 × 10-12
k (kilo)        103        5k = 5 × 103         f (femto)       10-15  0.2f = 0.2 × 10-15
m (milli)       10-3       -3.2m = -3.2 × 10-3  a (atto)        10-18  2a = 2 × 10-18
